\displaystyle=-{\cal A}P\!\!\!\!/\penalty u\!\!\!/\penalty-{\cal B}.

(308b)

Taking trace of (
308a
) and (
308b
), we get

\displaystyle{\rm{Tr}}\left[\Sigma P\!\!\!\!/\penalty\,\right]

\displaystyle=-4{\cal A}P^{2}-4{\cal B}\left(P\cdot u\right),

(309a)

\displaystyle\left(P\cdot u\right){\rm{Tr}}\left[\Sigma u\!\!\!/\penalty\right]

\displaystyle=-4{\cal A}\left(P\cdot u\right)^{2}-4{\cal B}\left(P\cdot u\right).

(309b)

Solving (
309a
) and (
309b
), one obtains

{\cal A}(\omega,p)=\frac{1}{4}\frac{{\rm{Tr}}\left[\Sigma P\!\!\!\!/\penalty\,\right]-\left(P\cdot u\right){\rm{Tr}}\left[\Sigma u\!\!\!/\penalty\right]}{\left(P\cdot u\right)^{2}-P^{2}}.

(310)

Further one can write

\displaystyle P^{2}{\rm{Tr}}\left[\Sigma u\!\!\!/\penalty\right]

\displaystyle=-4{\cal A}P^{2}\left(P\cdot u\right)-4{\cal B}P^{2},

(311a)

\displaystyle\left(P\cdot u\right){\rm{Tr}}\left[\Sigma P\!\!\!\!/\penalty\,\right]

\displaystyle=-4{\cal A}P^{2}\left(P\cdot u\right)-4{\cal B}\left(P\cdot u\right)^{2}.

(311b)

Solving (
311a
) and (
311b
), one obtains

{\cal B}(\omega,p)=\frac{1}{4}\frac{P^{2}{\rm{Tr}}\left[\Sigma u\!\!\!/\penalty\right]-\left(P\cdot u\right){\rm{Tr}}\left[\Sigma P\!\!\!\!/\penalty\,\right]}{\left(P\cdot u\right)^{2}-P^{2}}.

(312)

Now in the rest frame of the heat bath,
u^{\mu}=(1,0,0,0)
, the most general ansatz for fermionic self-energy reads
HAW
as

\displaystyle\Sigma(P)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-{\cal A}(\omega,p)P\!\!\!\!/\penalty-{\cal B}(\omega,p)\gamma_{0},

(313)

with structure functions

\displaystyle{\cal A}(\omega,p)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4p^{2}}\left({\rm{Tr}}\left[\Sigma P\!\!\!\!/\penalty\,\right]-\omega{\rm{Tr}}\left[\Sigma\gamma_{0}\right]\right),

(314a)

\displaystyle{\cal B}(\omega,p)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4p^{2}}\left(P^{2}{\rm{Tr}}\left[\Sigma\gamma_{0}\right]-\omega{\rm{Tr}}\left[\Sigma P\!\!\!\!/\penalty\,\right]\right).

(314b)

7.2
Fermion Propagator

Figure 23:
Pictorial representation of Dyson-Schwinger equation for effective fermion propagator.

In Fig.
23
we represent the full propagator by
S^{*}(P)
and the bare propagator by
S_{0}(P)
and self-energy as
\Sigma(P)
, then the full propagator is written as,

\displaystyle S^{*}(P)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{0}(P)+S_{0}(P)\Sigma(P)S^{*}(P)

\displaystyle S^{*}(P)S^{*-1}(P)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{0}(P)S^{*-1}(P)+S_{0}(P)\Sigma(P)S^{*}(P)S^{*-1}(P)

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{0}(P)S^{*-1}(P)+S_{0}(P)\Sigma(P)

\displaystyle S_{0}^{-1}(P)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{0}^{-1}(P)S_{0}(P)S^{*-1}(P)+S_{0}^{-1}(P)S_{0}(P)\Sigma(P)

\displaystyle S^{*-1}(P)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{0}^{-1}(P)-\Sigma(P)

\displaystyle S^{*-1}(P)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\not{P}-\Sigma(P)\,,

(315)

which is known as fermionic Dyson-Schwinger equation.
The effective fermion propagator can be obtained as

S^{\star}(P)=\frac{1}{P\!\!\!\!/\penalty-\Sigma(P)}.

(316)

Using (
307
) one can write the effective propagator as

S^{\star}(P)=\frac{1}{(1+{\cal A})P\!\!\!\!/\penalty+{\cal B}u\!\!\!/\penalty}=\frac{(1+{\cal A})P\!\!\!\!/\penalty+{\cal B}u\!\!\!/\penalty}{[(1+{\cal A})P\!\!\!\!/\penalty+{\cal B}u\!\!\!/\penalty]^{2}}=\frac{P\!\!\!\!/\penalty-\Sigma(P)}{\cal D}=\frac{S^{\star-1}(P)}{\cal D},

(317)

where the Lorentz invariant quantity
{\cal D}
is given as

\displaystyle{\cal D}{(p,u)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[(1+{\cal A})P\!\!\!\!/\penalty+{\cal B}u\!\!\!/\penalty\right]^{2}=(1+{\cal A})^{2}{P\!\!\!\!/\penalty}^{2}+2(1+{\cal A}){\cal B}P\cdot u+{\cal B}^{2}.

(318)

In the rest frame of heat bath, Eq.(
318
) reads as

\displaystyle{\cal D}{(p,\omega)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1+{\cal A})^{2}(\omega^{2}-p^{2})+2(1+{\cal A}){\cal B}\omega+{\cal B}^{2}=\left[(1+{\cal A})\omega+{\cal B}\right]^{2}-(1+{\cal A})^{2}p^{2}

(319)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[(1+{\cal A})(\omega-p)+{\cal B}\right]\left[(1+{\cal A})(\omega+p)+{\cal B}\right]={\cal D}_{+}{\cal D}_{-},

where

{\cal D}_{\pm}(p,\omega)=(1+{\cal A})(\omega\mp p)+{\cal B}.

(320)

In free case
{\cal A}={\cal B}=0
and Eq.(
320
) becomes

d_{\pm}(p,\omega)=\omega\mp p.

(321)

Combining (
319
) and (
317
), one can write the effective propagator as

S^{\star}(P)=\frac{S^{\star-1}(P)}{{\cal D}_{+}{\cal D}_{-}}.

(322)

We can write the self energy in (
313
) as

\displaystyle\Sigma(P)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-{\cal A}(\omega,p)P\!\!\!\!/\penalty-{\cal B}(\omega,p)\gamma_{0}

(323)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-({\cal A}\omega+{\cal B})\gamma_{0}+{\cal A}p\ {\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left[-({\cal A}\omega+{\cal B})\gamma_{0}-({\cal A}\omega+{\cal B})\gamma_{0}-{\cal A}p\gamma_{0}+{\cal A}p\gamma_{0}+{\cal A}p\ {\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}}+{\cal A}p\ {\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}}\right.

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \left.-({\cal A}\omega+{\cal B}){\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}}+({\cal A}\omega+{\cal B}){\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left[-({\cal A}\omega+{\cal B})(\gamma_{0}-{\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}})-({\cal A}\omega+{\cal B})(\gamma_{0}+{\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}})-{\cal A}p(\gamma_{0}-{\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}})+{\cal A}p(\gamma_{0}+{\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\left[\left({\cal A}(\omega+p)+{\cal B}\right)(\gamma_{0}-{\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}})+\left({\cal A}(\omega-p)+{\cal B}\right)(\gamma_{0}+{\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}})\right].

Now we can write

\displaystyle P\!\!\!\!/\penalty

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\gamma_{0}\omega-p{\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}}=\frac{1}{2}\left[\gamma_{0}\omega+\gamma_{0}\omega-\gamma_{0}p+\gamma_{0}p-p{\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}}-p{\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}}+\omega{\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}}-\omega{\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}}\right]

(324)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left[(\omega-p)(\gamma_{0}+{\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}})+(\omega+p)(\gamma_{0}-{\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}})\right].

In the rest frame of heat bath the inverse of the effective propagator in (
315
) can now be written as

\displaystyle S^{\star-1}(P)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{P\!\!\!\!/\penalty-\Sigma(P)}

(325)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left[\left\{(\omega-p)+{\cal A}(\omega-p)+{\cal B}\right\}(\gamma_{0}+{\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}})+\left\{(\omega+p)+{\cal A}(\omega+p)+{\cal B}\right\}(\gamma_{0}-{\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left[(1+{\cal A})(\omega-p)+{\cal B}\right](\gamma_{0}+{\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}})+\frac{1}{2}\left[(1+{\cal A})(\omega+p)+{\cal B}\right](\gamma_{0}-{\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(\gamma_{0}+{\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}}){\cal D}_{+}+\frac{1}{2}(\gamma_{0}-{\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}}){\cal D}_{-}

Using (
325
) in (
322
), one finally obtains the effective fermion propagator as

S^{\star}(P)=\frac{1}{2}\frac{(\gamma_{0}-{\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}})}{{\cal D}_{+}(\omega,p)}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{(\gamma_{0}+{\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}})}{{\cal D}_{-}(\omega,p)},

(326)

which is decomposed in helicity eigenstates.

In free fermion case, the propagator becomes

S(P)=\frac{1}{2}\frac{(\gamma_{0}-{\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}})}{d_{+}(\omega,p)}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{(\gamma_{0}+{\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}})}{d_{-}(\omega,p)},

(327)

where
d_{\pm}(\omega,p)
are given in (
321
) and has already been used in Sec.
6.3.3
in (
298
).

8
General structure of Gauge Boson Two-point Functions at
T\neq 0

8.1
Covariant Description

.

The general structure of the gauge boson self-energy in vacuum
Peskin
is given as

\displaystyle\Pi^{\mu\nu}(P^{2})=V^{\mu\nu}\Pi(P^{2}),

(328)

where the form factor
\Pi(P^{2})
is Lorentz invariant and
depends only on the four scalar
P^{2}
. The vacuum projection operator is given by

\displaystyle V^{\mu\nu}=\eta^{\mu\nu}-\frac{P^{\mu}P^{\nu}}{P^{2}},

(329)

which satisfies the gauge invariance through the transversality condition

\displaystyle P_{\mu}\Pi^{\mu\nu}=0,

(330)

with
\eta^{\mu\nu}\equiv(1,-1,-1,-1)
and
P\equiv(\omega,\bm{\vec{p}})
. It is also symmetric under the exchange of
\mu\leftrightarrow\nu
as

\Pi_{\mu\nu}(P^{2})=\Pi_{\nu\mu}(P^{2}).

(331)

The presence of the heat bath or the finite temperature (
\beta=1/T
) breaks the Lorentz invariance
of the system. One collects all the four vectors
and tensors in order to construct a general covariant structure of the gauge boson
self-energy at finite temperature. These are

P^{\mu}
and
\eta^{\mu\nu}
from vacuum, and the four-velocity
u^{\mu}
of the heat bath. With these one can form four types of tensors,
namely
P^{\mu}P^{\nu},P^{\mu}u^{\nu}+u^{\mu}P^{\nu},u^{\mu}u^{\nu}
and
\eta^{\mu\nu}

Ashok_Das
;
Weldon:1982aq
. These
four tensors can form two independent tensors by virtue of two constraints provided by
the transversality condition in (
330
). One can form two
mutually orthogonal projection tensors from these two independent tensors in order to construct
Lorentz-invariant structure of the gauge boson two point functions
at finite temperature.

Now, we define the Lorentz scalars, vectors and tensors that characterise
the heat bath:

\displaystyle u^{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1,0,0,0),

\displaystyle P^{\mu}u_{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P\cdot u=\omega,

(332)

8.2
Tensor Decomposition

Similar to vaccum, we can define
\tilde{\eta}^{\mu\nu}
transverse to
u^{\mu}
as

\displaystyle\tilde{\eta}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\eta^{\mu\nu}-u^{\mu}u^{\nu}\,

(333a)

\displaystyle u_{\mu}\tilde{\eta}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=u_{\mu}\eta^{\mu\nu}-u_{\mu}u^{\mu}u^{\nu}=u^{\nu}-u^{\nu}=0\,.

(333b)

So
u^{\mu}
and
\tilde{\eta}^{\mu\nu}
are transverse.

Any four vector can be decomposed parallel and orthogonal component with respect to
u^{\mu}
:

\displaystyle P^{\mu}_{\shortparallel}

\displaystyle=(P\cdot u)u^{\mu}=\omega u^{\mu},

(334a)

\displaystyle P^{\mu}_{\perp}

\displaystyle=\tilde{P}^{\mu}=P^{\mu}-P^{\mu}_{\shortparallel}=P^{\mu}-\omega u^{\mu}\,.

(334b)

Now,

\displaystyle\tilde{P}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(P^{\mu}-\omega u^{\mu}\right)\left(P_{\mu}-\omega u_{\mu}\right)=P^{2}-\omega^{2}-\omega^{2}+\omega^{2}=P^{2}-\omega^{2}=-p^{2}\,.

(335)

We can also define any four vector parallel and perpendicular to
P^{\mu}

\displaystyle u^{\mu}_{\shortparallel}

\displaystyle=\frac{(P\cdot u)P^{\mu}}{P^{2}}=\frac{\omega P^{\mu}}{P^{2}}\,,

(336a)

\displaystyle\bar{u}^{\mu}

\displaystyle\equiv u^{\mu}_{\perp}=u^{\mu}-u^{\mu}_{\shortparallel}=u^{\mu}-\frac{\omega P^{\mu}}{P^{2}}\,.

(336b)

So,
P^{\mu}\bar{u}_{\mu}=0
.

Again,

\displaystyle V^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\eta^{\mu\nu}-\frac{P^{\mu}P^{\nu}}{P^{2}}\,,

(337a)

\displaystyle P_{\mu}V^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=0\,.

(337b)

Given these, it is possible to construct only two independent second rank symmetric tensors at finite
temperature from
\eta^{\mu\nu},P^{\mu}P^{\nu},u^{\mu}u^{\nu},P^{\mu}u^{\nu}+u^{\mu}P^{\nu}
which are orthogonal to
P^{\mu}
.
These two tensors are
Ashok_Das

A^{\mu\nu}=\tilde{\eta}^{\mu\nu}-\frac{\tilde{P}^{\mu}\tilde{P}^{\nu}}{\tilde{P}^{2}}\,,

(338)

and

B^{\mu\nu}=\frac{P^{2}}{\tilde{P}^{2}}\bar{u}^{\mu}\bar{u}^{\nu}=\frac{\bar{u}^{\mu}\bar{u}^{\nu}}{\bar{u}^{2}}\,,

(339)

where

A^{\mu\nu}+B^{\mu\nu}=V^{\mu\nu}=\eta^{\mu\nu}-\frac{P^{\mu}P^{\nu}}{P^{2}}\,.

(340)

We show that
A^{\mu\nu}
and
B^{\mu\nu}
are orthogonal to
P^{\mu}
:

\displaystyle P_{\mu}A^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{\mu}\tilde{\eta}^{\mu\nu}-P_{\mu}\frac{\tilde{P}^{\mu}\tilde{P}^{\nu}}{\tilde{P}^{2}}

(341)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{\mu}\left(\eta^{\mu\nu}-u^{\mu}u^{\nu}\right)-\frac{P_{\mu}\left(P^{\mu}-\omega u^{\mu}\right)\tilde{P}^{\nu}}{\tilde{P}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(P^{\nu}-\omega u^{\nu}\right)-\left(P^{2}-\omega^{2}\right)\frac{\tilde{P}^{\nu}}{\tilde{P}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{P}^{\nu}-\frac{\tilde{P}^{2}\tilde{P}^{\nu}}{\tilde{P}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0\,.

\displaystyle P_{\mu}B^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{P^{2}}{\tilde{P}^{2}}P_{\mu}\left(u^{\mu}-\frac{\omega P^{\mu}}{P^{2}}\right)\left(u^{\nu}-\frac{\omega P^{\nu}}{P^{2}}\right)

(342)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{P^{2}}{\tilde{P}^{2}}\left[\left(\omega-\frac{\omega P^{2}}{P^{2}}\right)\left(u^{\nu}-\frac{\omega P^{\nu}}{P^{2}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0\,.

A^{\mu\nu}
and
B^{\mu\nu}
also satisfy following relations:

\displaystyle A^{\mu\nu}B_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\left(\tilde{\eta}_{\mu\nu}-\frac{\tilde{P}_{\mu}\tilde{P}_{\nu}}{\tilde{P}^{2}}\right)\frac{P^{2}}{\tilde{P}^{2}}\bar{u}^{\mu}\bar{u}^{\nu}=0\,

(343a)

\displaystyle A_{\mu\nu}A^{\nu\rho}

\displaystyle=\left(\tilde{\eta}_{\mu\rho}-\frac{\tilde{P}_{\mu}\tilde{P}_{\rho}}{\tilde{P}^{2}}\right)\left(\tilde{\eta}^{\rho\nu}-\frac{\tilde{P}^{\rho}\tilde{P}^{\nu}}{\tilde{P}^{2}}\right)=A_{\mu}^{\rho}\,,

(343b)

\displaystyle B_{\mu\nu}B^{\nu\rho}

\displaystyle=\left(V_{\mu\rho}-A_{\mu\rho}\right)\left(V^{\rho\nu}-A^{\rho\nu}\right)

\displaystyle=V_{\mu\rho}V^{\rho\nu}-2A_{\mu\rho}V^{\rho\nu}+A_{\mu\rho}A^{\rho\nu}

\displaystyle=V_{\mu}^{\nu}-2A_{\mu}^{\nu}+A_{\mu}^{\nu}=B_{\mu}^{\rho}\,,

(343c)

\displaystyle A^{\mu\nu}A_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=2\,,

(343d)

\displaystyle B^{\mu\nu}B_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=1\,,

(343e)

\displaystyle A_{\mu\nu}(P)

\displaystyle=A_{\nu\mu}(P)=A_{\mu\nu}(-P),

(343f)

\displaystyle B_{\mu\nu}(P)

\displaystyle=B_{\nu\mu}(P)=B_{\mu\nu}(-P)\,.

(343g)

Finally, one can obtain

\displaystyle A^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\eta^{\mu\nu}-u^{\mu}u^{\nu}-\frac{\left(P^{\mu}-\omega u^{\mu}\right)\left(P^{\nu}-\omega u^{\nu}\right)}{P^{2}-\omega^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{P^{2}-\omega^{2}}\left[(P^{2}-\omega^{2})(\eta^{\mu\nu}-u^{\mu}u^{\nu})-P^{\mu}P^{\nu}-\omega^{2}u^{\mu}u^{\nu}+\omega(P^{\mu}u^{\nu}+u^{\mu}P^{\nu})\right]\,,

(344a)

\displaystyle B^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\frac{P^{2}}{\tilde{P}^{2}}\bar{u}^{\mu}\bar{u}^{\nu}=\frac{P^{2}}{P^{2}-\omega^{2}}\left[(u^{\mu}-\frac{\omega P^{\mu}}{P^{2}})(u^{\nu}-\frac{\omega P^{\nu}}{P^{2}})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{P^{2}}{P^{2}-\omega^{2}}\left[u^{\mu}u^{\nu}-\frac{\omega P^{\mu}u^{\nu}}{P^{2}}-\frac{\omega u^{\mu}P^{\nu}}{P^{2}}+\frac{\omega^{2}P^{\mu}P^{\nu}}{P^{4}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{P^{2}\left(P^{2}-\omega^{2}\right)}\left[P^{4}u^{\mu}u^{\nu}+\omega^{2}P^{\mu}P^{\nu}-\omega P^{2}(P^{\mu}u^{\nu}+u^{\mu}P^{\nu})\right]\,.

(344b)

8.3
General Structure of Self-energy of a Vector Particle in a Thermal Medium

The self-energy of a vector particle in a medium (finite temperature/density) can be written as

\Pi_{\mu\nu}=\Pi_{T}(\omega,p)A_{\mu\nu}+\Pi_{L}(\omega,p)B_{\mu\nu},

(345)

It obeys the current conservation or transversality condition as

\displaystyle P^{\mu}\Pi_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Pi_{T}P^{\mu}A_{\mu\nu}+\Pi_{L}P^{\mu}B_{\mu\nu}

(346)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0\,.

Also note that at zero temperature

\Pi^{(0)}_{\mu\nu}(\omega,p)=\Pi^{(0)}(P^{2})\left(\eta_{\mu\nu}-\frac{P_{\mu}P_{\nu}}{P^{2}}\right)\,,

(347)

where

\Pi^{(0)}_{L}(\omega,p)=\Pi^{(0)}_{T}(\omega,p)=\Pi^{(0)}(P^{2})\,.

(348)

Using (
345
) we can write

\displaystyle\Pi_{00}(\omega,p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Pi_{T}(\omega,p)A_{00}+\Pi_{L}(\omega,p)B_{00}\,.

(349)

We obtain from (
344a
) and (
344b
), respectively, as

\displaystyle A_{00}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(350a)

\displaystyle B_{00}

\displaystyle=-\frac{p^{2}}{P^{2}}\,.

(350b)

Using (
350a
) and (
350b
) in (
349
), one obtains
Ashok_Das

\displaystyle\Pi_{L}(\omega,p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(-\frac{P^{2}}{p^{2}}\right)\Pi_{00}(\omega,p)\,.

(351)

Again, we can write

\eta^{\mu\nu}\Pi_{\mu\nu}=\Pi_{T}\eta^{\mu\nu}A_{\mu\nu}+\Pi_{L}\eta^{\mu\nu}B_{\mu\nu}\,.

(352)

One obtains

\displaystyle\eta^{\mu\nu}A_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta^{\mu\nu}A_{\mu\nu}-u^{\mu}u_{\mu}-\frac{\tilde{P}^{\mu}\tilde{P}_{\nu}}{\tilde{P}^{2}}

\displaystyle=D-1-1=D-2,

(353)

where
D=4
is the dimension of the system. Also, one can obtain

\displaystyle\eta^{\mu\nu}B_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta^{\mu\nu}\frac{P^{2}}{\tilde{P}^{2}}\bar{u}_{\mu}\bar{u}_{\nu}=\frac{P^{2}}{\tilde{P}^{2}}\bar{u}_{\mu}\bar{u}^{\mu}=\frac{P^{2}}{\tilde{P}^{2}}\left(u_{\mu}-\frac{\omega P_{\mu}}{P^{2}}\right)\left(u^{\mu}-\frac{\omega P^{\mu}}{P^{2}}\right)

(354)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{P^{2}}{\tilde{P}^{2}}\left[u_{\mu}u^{\mu}-\frac{\omega}{P^{2}}\left(P_{\mu}u^{\mu}+P^{\mu}u_{\mu}\right)+\frac{\omega^{2}}{P^{4}}P_{\mu}P^{\mu}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{P^{2}}{\tilde{P}^{2}}\left(1-\frac{2\omega^{2}}{P^{2}}+\frac{\omega^{2}}{P^{2}}\right)=\frac{P^{2}}{P^{2}-\omega^{2}}\left(\frac{P^{2}-\omega^{2}}{P^{2}}\right)=1\,.

Equation (
353
) denotes the presence of two transverse modes whereas (
354
) corresponds to a longitudinal mode of the gauge boson.

Using (
353
) and (
354
) in (
352
) we obtain
Ashok_Das

\Pi_{T}(\omega,p)=\frac{1}{D-2}\left[{\Pi}^{\mu}_{\mu}(\omega,p)-\Pi_{L}(\omega,p)\right]=\frac{1}{2}\left[{\Pi}^{\mu}_{\mu}(\omega,p)-\Pi_{L}(\omega,p)\right].

(355)

8.4
Massless Vector Gauge Boson Propagator in Covariant Gauge

Figure 24:
Effective gauge boson propagator.

We represent the full propagator by
D_{mn}
and the bare propagator by
D^{0}_{\mu\nu}
and each blob that appears in the summation as
\Pi_{\mu\nu}
.
One can work out this summation in Fig.
24
in tensorial form for the effective gauge boson propagator in Fig.
24
as

\displaystyle D^{\mu\rho}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{D^{0}}^{\mu\rho}+{D^{0}}^{\mu\alpha}\Pi_{\alpha\beta}{D}^{\beta\rho}

\displaystyle D^{\mu\rho}D^{-1}_{\rho\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{D^{0}}^{\mu\rho}D^{-1}_{\rho\nu}+{D^{0}}^{\mu\alpha}\Pi_{\alpha\beta}D^{\beta\rho}D^{-1}_{\rho\nu}

\displaystyle\delta^{\mu}_{\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{D^{0}}^{\mu\rho}D^{-1}_{\rho\nu}+{D^{0}}^{\mu\alpha}\Pi_{\alpha\beta}\delta^{\beta}_{\nu}

\displaystyle\delta^{\mu}_{\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{D^{0}}^{\mu\rho}D^{-1}_{\rho\nu}+{D^{0}}^{\mu\alpha}\Pi_{\alpha\nu}

\displaystyle\delta^{\mu}_{\nu}(D^{0}_{\mu\gamma})^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{D^{0}}^{\mu\rho}(D^{0}_{\mu\gamma})^{-1}D^{-1}_{\rho\nu}+{D^{0}}^{\mu\alpha}(D^{0}_{\mu\gamma})^{-1}\Pi_{\alpha\nu}

\displaystyle(D^{0}_{\nu\gamma})^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta^{\rho}_{\gamma}D^{-1}_{\rho\nu}+\delta^{\alpha}_{\gamma}\Pi_{\alpha\nu}

\displaystyle D^{-1}_{\nu\gamma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(D^{0}_{\nu\gamma})^{-1}-\Pi_{\nu\gamma}.

(356)

Effective photon propagator is given by

{D^{-1}_{\mu\nu}=(D^{0}_{\mu\nu})^{-1}-\Pi_{\mu\nu}}\,,

(357)

which is known as Dyson-Schwinger equation.

For massless gauge boson propagator is given in (
414
) as

D^{0}_{\mu\nu}=-\frac{\eta_{\mu\nu}}{P^{2}}+{(1-\xi)}\frac{P_{\mu}P_{\nu}}{P^{4}}\,

(358)

where
\xi
is the gauge fixing parameter with
\xi=1
in Feynman gauge and
\xi=0
in Landau gauge. We will discuss the gauge fixing and gauge boson propagator
in subsec
9.4
.

Dyson-Schwinger equation is given in (
357
) as

D^{-1}_{\mu\nu}=(D^{0}_{\mu\nu})^{-1}-\Pi_{\mu\nu}\,.

(359)

So we have to calculate the inverse of
D^{0}_{\mu\nu}
.

Lets define

(D^{0}_{\mu\rho})^{-1}=a\eta_{\mu\rho}+bP_{\mu}P_{\rho}\,.

(360)

We know

\displaystyle D^{0}_{\mu\rho}({D^{0}}^{\rho\nu})^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{\mu}^{\nu}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{P^{2}}\left(-\eta_{\mu\rho}+(1-\xi)\frac{P_{\mu}P_{\rho}}{P^{2}}\right)\left(a\eta^{\rho\nu}+bP^{\rho}P^{\nu}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{\mu}^{\nu}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{P^{2}}\left(-a\delta_{\mu}^{\nu}+\frac{a(1-\xi)}{P^{2}}P_{\mu}P^{\nu}-bP_{\mu}P^{\nu}+b(1-\xi){P_{\mu}P^{\nu}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{\mu}^{\nu}\,.

(361)

Equating coefficients of both side, we get

{a=-P^{2}}\,\,,\,\,{b=-\frac{1-\xi}{\xi}}\,.

(362)

Using (
362
) in (
360
), one obtains

(D^{0}_{\mu\nu})^{-1}=-P^{2}\eta_{\mu\nu}-\frac{1-\xi}{\xi}P_{\mu}P_{\nu}.

(363)

We know from (
340
)

\displaystyle P_{\mu}P_{\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P^{2}(\eta_{\mu\nu}-A_{\mu\nu}-B_{\mu\nu})\,.

(364)

Substituting this in
(D^{0}_{\mu\nu})^{-1}
, one can have

\displaystyle(D^{0}_{\mu\nu})^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-P^{2}\eta_{\mu\nu}-\frac{1-\xi}{\xi}P^{2}(\eta_{\mu\nu}-A_{\mu\nu}-B_{\mu\nu})

(365)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{P^{2}}{\xi}\eta_{\mu\nu}+\frac{1-\xi}{\xi}P^{2}(A_{\mu\nu}+B_{\mu\nu})\,,

which is the inverse of
D^{0}_{\mu\nu}
.

Using (
365
) and (
345
) in (
357
) one can find

\displaystyle D_{\mu\nu}^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{P^{2}}{\xi}\eta_{\mu\nu}+\frac{1-\xi}{\xi}P^{2}(A_{\mu\nu}+B_{\mu\nu})-\Pi_{T}A_{\mu\nu}-\Pi_{L}B_{\mu\nu}

(366)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{P^{2}}{\xi}\eta_{\mu\nu}+(P_{m}^{2}-\Pi_{T})A_{\mu\nu}+(P_{m}^{2}-\Pi_{L})B_{\mu\nu}\,,

where
P_{m}^{2}=\frac{(1-\xi)}{\xi}P^{2}
. Eq.(
366
) is inverse of
D_{\mu\nu}
.
Now we have to find out
D_{\mu\nu}
from
D_{\mu\nu}^{-1}
.

Lets define

D_{\mu\rho}=cP_{\mu}P_{\rho}+dA_{\mu\rho}+eB_{\mu\rho}\,.

(367)

We know

D_{\mu\rho}(D^{\rho\nu})^{-1}=\delta_{\mu}^{\nu}\,

\frac{cP^{2}}{\xi}P_{\mu}P^{\nu}+d\left(P^{2}+\Pi_{T}\right)A_{\mu}^{\nu}+e\left(P^{2}+\Pi_{L}\right)B_{\mu}^{\nu}=-\delta_{\mu}^{\nu}\,.

(368)

Substituting
A_{\mu}^{\nu}
and
B_{\mu}^{\nu}
from (
344a
) and (
344b
), the above equation becomes

\displaystyle\frac{cP^{2}}{\xi}P_{\mu}P^{\nu}+d\left(P^{2}+\Pi_{T}\ \right)\left[\delta_{\mu}^{\nu}-\frac{P^{2}}{P^{2}-\omega^{2}}u_{\mu}u^{\nu}-\frac{P_{\mu}P^{\nu}}{P^{2}-\omega^{2}}+\frac{\omega}{P^{2}-\omega^{2}}\left(u_{\mu}P^{\nu}+P_{\mu}u^{\nu}\right)\right]

(369)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle e\left(P^{2}+\Pi_{L}\right)\frac{P^{2}}{P^{2}-\omega^{2}}\left[u_{\mu}u^{\nu}-\frac{\omega}{P^{2}}\left(u_{\mu}P^{\nu}+P_{\mu}u^{\nu}\right)+\frac{\omega^{2}}{P^{4}}P_{\mu}P^{\nu}\right]=-\delta_{\mu}^{\nu}\,.

Now equating coefficients on both side, we get

\displaystyle{\mbox{Coefficients of}}\,\,\delta_{\mu}^{\nu}:\hskip 56.9055ptd

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{P^{2}+\Pi_{T}}\,,

(370a)

\displaystyle{\mbox{Coefficients of}}\,\,u_{\mu}P^{\nu}+P_{\mu}u^{\nu}:\hskip 56.9055pte

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{P^{2}+\Pi_{L}}\,,

(370b)

\displaystyle{\mbox{Coefficients of}}\,\,P_{\mu}P^{\nu}:\hskip 56.9055ptc

\displaystyle=-\frac{\xi}{P^{4}}\,.

(370c)

Using (
370a
), (
370b
) and (
370c
) in (
367
), one obtains the effective propagator of interacting photon
Ashok_Das
in presence of thermal medium as

{D_{\mu\nu}=-\frac{\xi}{P^{4}}P_{\mu}P_{\nu}-\frac{1}{P^{2}+\Pi_{T}}A_{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{P^{2}+\Pi_{L}}B_{\mu\nu}}\,.

(371)

8.5
Massive Vector Boson Propagator

The free propagator for massive vector boson is given as

\displaystyle D^{0}_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-\eta_{\mu\nu}+\frac{P_{\mu}P_{\nu}}{m_{v}^{2}}}{P^{2}-m_{v}^{2}}

(372)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{-\eta_{\mu\nu}+\frac{P_{\mu}P_{\nu}}{m_{v}^{2}}}{X}\,,

where
X=P^{2}-m_{v}^{2}
and
m_{v}
is the mass of the vector boson.

The inverse of
D^{0}_{\mu\nu}
can be written as

(D^{0}_{\rho\nu})^{-1}=a\eta_{\rho\nu}+bP_{\rho}P_{\nu}\,.

(373)

We have

\displaystyle D^{0}_{\mu\rho}{(D^{0\rho\nu}})^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{\mu}^{\nu}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{-\eta_{\mu\rho}+\frac{P_{\mu}P_{\rho}}{m_{v}^{2}}}{X}\right)\left(a\eta^{\rho\nu}+bP^{\rho}P^{\nu}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{\mu}^{\nu}

\displaystyle-a\delta_{\mu}^{\nu}-bP_{\mu}P^{\nu}+a\frac{P_{\mu}P^{\nu}}{m_{v}^{2}}+b\frac{P^{2}P_{\mu}P^{\nu}}{m_{v}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle X\delta_{\mu}^{\nu}\,.

(374)

Equating coefficients of
\delta_{\mu}^{\nu},
and
P_{\mu}P^{\nu}
yields,

a=-X=-(P^{2}-m_{v}^{2})\,,

(375)

and

\displaystyle\frac{a}{m_{v}^{2}}-b+\frac{bP^{2}}{m_{v}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle b

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1\,.

(376)

Now the inverse of the free propagator in (
373
) becomes

(D^{0}_{\mu\nu})^{-1}=-(P^{2}-m_{v}^{2})\eta_{\mu\nu}+P_{\mu}P_{\nu}\,.

(377)

Using (
340
) we get

P_{\mu}P_{\nu}=P^{2}\left(\eta_{\mu\nu}-A_{\mu\nu}-B_{\mu\nu}\right)\,,

(378)

one gets

\displaystyle(D^{0}_{\mu\nu})^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(P^{2}-m_{v}^{2})\eta_{\mu\nu}+P^{2}\left(\eta_{\mu\nu}-A_{\mu\nu}-B_{\mu\nu}\right)

(379)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m_{v}^{2}\eta_{\mu\nu}-P^{2}\left(A_{\mu\nu}+B_{\mu\nu}\right)\,.

Putting back this value in Dyson equation in (
357
)

D_{\mu\nu}^{-1}=m_{v}^{2}\eta_{\mu\nu}-P^{2}\left(A_{\mu\nu}+B_{\mu\nu}\right)-\Pi_{\mu\nu}\,.

(380)

Using Eq.(
345
) we get

\displaystyle D_{\mu\nu}^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m_{v}^{2}\eta_{\mu\nu}-P^{2}\left(A_{\mu\nu}+B_{\mu\nu}\right)-\Pi_{T}A_{\mu\nu}-\Pi_{L}B_{\mu\nu}

(381)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m_{v}^{2}\eta_{\mu\nu}-\left(P^{2}+\Pi_{T}\right)A_{\mu\nu}-\left(P^{2}+\Pi_{L}\right)B_{\mu\nu}

Now we have to find
D_{\mu\nu}
. Lets define

D^{\rho\nu}=\alpha P^{\rho}P^{\nu}+\beta A^{\rho\nu}+\gamma B^{\rho\nu}\,.

(382)

We can write

\displaystyle D_{\mu\rho}(D^{\rho\nu})^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{\mu}^{\nu}

\displaystyle\left(\alpha P_{\mu}P_{\rho}+\beta A_{\mu\rho}+\gamma B_{\mu\rho}\right)\left(m_{v}^{2}\eta^{\rho\nu}-P^{2}\left(A^{\rho\nu}+B^{\rho\nu}\right)-\Pi_{T}A^{\rho\nu}-\Pi_{L}B^{\rho\nu}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{\mu}^{\nu}

\displaystyle\alpha m_{v}^{2}P_{\mu}P^{\nu}+\beta m_{v}^{2}A_{\mu}^{\nu}-\beta\left(P^{2}+\Pi_{T}\right)A_{\mu}^{\nu}+\gamma m_{v}^{2}B_{\mu}^{\nu}-\gamma\left(P^{2}+\Pi_{L}\right)B_{\mu}^{\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{\mu}^{\nu}

\displaystyle\alpha m_{v}^{2}P_{\mu}P^{\nu}+\beta\left(m_{v}^{2}-P^{2}-\Pi_{T}\right)A_{\mu}^{\nu}+\gamma\left(m_{v}^{2}-P^{2}-\Pi_{L}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{\mu}^{\nu}\,.

(383)

Substituting
A_{\mu}^{\nu},B_{\mu}^{\nu}
from Eq.(
344a
),Eq.(
344b
) and equating coefficients we get,

\displaystyle{\mbox{Coefficients of}\,\,\,\delta_{\mu}^{\nu}}:\hskip 56.9055pt\beta

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m_{v}^{2}-P^{2}-\Pi_{T}}\,,

(384a)

\displaystyle{\mbox{Coefficients of}\,\,\,u_{\mu}u^{\nu}}:\hskip 56.9055pt\gamma

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m_{v}^{2}-P^{2}-\Pi_{L}}\,,

(384b)

\displaystyle{\mbox{Coefficients of}\,\,\,P_{\mu}P^{\nu}}:\hskip 56.9055pt\alpha

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{P^{2}m_{v}^{2}}\,.

(384c)

Using (
384a
) to (
384c
) in (
382
) one gets the propagator for massive vector boson in a thermal medium

D_{\mu\nu}=\frac{P_{\mu}P_{\nu}}{P^{2}m_{v}^{2}}-\frac{A_{\mu\nu}}{{P^{2}-m_{v}^{2}+\Pi_{T}}}-\frac{B_{\mu\nu}}{P^{2}-m_{v}^{2}+\Pi_{L}}\,.

(385)

9
Quantum Electrodynamics (QED)

Quantum electrodynamics (QED) is an abelian gauge theory. The symmetry group is
U(1)

abelian group which are also commutative group. In QED, the interaction between two spin
1/2

fermionic fields is mediated by electromagnetic field photon,
A_{\mu}
,
which is a gauge field. Before doing anything else it is essential to introduce gauge and gauge fixing first.

9.1
Dirac Field

The Dirac Lagrangian density in (
245
) describes the free fermion and given as

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{D}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\bar{\psi}}\left(i\partial\!\!\!/\penalty-m\right)\psi.

(386)

As we have seen in subsec.
6.3.1
that it is invariant under a global phase transformation,
e^{-ie\alpha}\psi(X)
, with a fixed
phase parameter which does not depend upon space and time. This is a global transformation.
If the phase factor
\alpha
is any differentiable function of space-time,
\alpha(X)
, i.e.,
at each space-time point it is different, then the transformation,

\psi(X)\rightarrow e^{-ie\alpha(X)}\psi(X),

(387)

is called local transformation.
The Lagrangian density in (
386
) is no longer invariant under such local
transformation as seen in (
263
):

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{D}\rightarrow{\cal L}^{\prime}_{D}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{\psi}e^{ie\alpha(X)}\left(i\partial\!\!\!/\penalty-m\right)\psi e^{-ie\alpha(X)}

(388)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{\psi}\left(i\partial\!\!\!/\penalty-m\right)\psi\,+\,e\bar{\psi}\partial\!\!\!/\penalty\alpha(X)\psi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{D}\,+\,e\bar{\psi}\partial\!\!\!/\penalty\alpha(X)\psi.

One needs to include a gauge potential (field)
A_{\mu}
in the theory. As we will see below that
this gauge field
A_{\mu}
together with the original fermionic fields make the Lagrangian invariant under such
local phase transformation. This is also called local gauge transformation.

Under the local gauge transformation, the modified Dirac invariant Lagrangian in (
386
) now reads as

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{D}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\bar{\psi}}\left(i\partial\!\!\!/\penalty-m\right)\psi-e{\bar{\psi}}\gamma^{\mu}A_{\mu}\psi={\bar{\psi}}\left(iD\!\!\!\!/\penalty-m\right)\psi,

(389)

where the original partial differential operator is replaced by the covariant differential
operator

\displaystyle\partial_{\mu}\rightarrow D_{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{\mu}+ieA_{\mu},

(390)

along with the transformation of the gauge field as

\displaystyle A_{\mu}\rightarrow A_{\mu}+\partial_{\mu}\alpha(X).

(391)

[
Now lets check the invariance of (
389
) under local gauge transformation:
\psi(X)\rightarrow\psi^{\prime}=e^{-ie\alpha(X)}\psi

\displaystyle\bar{\psi}^{\prime}D_{\mu}\psi^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{\psi}^{\prime}[\partial_{\mu}+ieA_{\mu}]e^{-ie\alpha(X)}\psi

(392)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{\psi}^{\prime}e^{-ie\alpha(X)}\partial_{\mu}\psi-ie\bar{\psi}^{\prime}(\partial_{\mu}\alpha(X))e^{-ie\alpha(X)}\psi+ie\bar{\psi}^{\prime}A_{\mu}\psi e^{-ie\alpha(X)}.

If (
392
) vis-a-vis (
389
) is to be invariant under local transformation of the fermionic field

\psi(X)\rightarrow\psi^{\prime}=e^{-ie\alpha(X)}\psi
, the gauge field
has also to be transformed as
A_{\mu}\rightarrow A_{\mu}+\partial_{\mu}\alpha(X)
as given in (
391
). So

\displaystyle\bar{\psi}^{\prime}D_{\mu}\psi^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{\psi}^{\prime}e^{-ie\alpha(X)}\partial_{\mu}\psi-ie\bar{\psi}^{\prime}(\partial_{\mu}\alpha(X))e^{-ie\alpha(X)}\psi+ie\bar{\psi}^{\prime}(A_{\mu}+\partial_{\mu}\alpha(X))e^{-ie\alpha(X)}\psi

(393)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{\psi}(\partial_{\mu}+ieA_{\mu})=\bar{\psi}D_{\mu}\psi.

This suggests that (
389
) is invariant under local gauge transformation.
]

9.2
Pure Gauge Field

Following Maxwell’s equations both electric field
{\mathbf{E}}
and the magnetic
field
{\mathbf{B}}
can be written from pure gauge field
A_{\mu}
in a manifestly covariant form as

\displaystyle F_{\mu\nu}=\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu},

(394)

which is known as electromagnetic field tensor. This is a gauge invariant quantity
and also
antisymmetric,
F_{\mu\nu}=-F_{\nu\mu}
, under the exchange of the Lorentz
indices
\mu\leftrightarrow\nu
.
One can now construct a Lorentz scalar out of
F_{\mu\nu}
, which can be included in the Lagrangian density for
pure gauge field as

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}F_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu},

(395)

where the normalisation factor
1/4
is chosen in such a way that it gives the correct equation of motion for electromagnetic field.

9.3
Electromagnetic Lagrangian

Now one can write the total electromagnetic Lagrangian density
pbpal
;
Peskin
describing fermions, electromagnetic field and
interaction between them as

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{em}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{\gamma}+{\cal L}_{D}

(396)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}F_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu}+{\bar{\psi}}\left(iD\!\!\!\!/\penalty-m\right)\psi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}F_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu}+{\bar{\psi}}\left(i\gamma^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}-m\right)\psi-e\bar{\psi}\gamma^{\mu}\psi A_{\mu}.

Figure 25:
Electron-photon interaction vertex.

We note the following:

\bullet

The first term corresponds to the free Lagrangian density of a gauge field (photon)

\bullet

The second term is the Lagrangian density that describes the free fermions with mass
m
. Now if one wants to include
chemical potential
\mu
, one should follow as done in (
268
) in Subsec.
6.3.1
. The presence of the chemical potential is
like shifting the temporal component of the gauge field by
\partial_{0}-i\mu
.

\bullet

The third term originates from the local
U(1)
gauge symmetry and corresponds to the interaction Lagrangian density of fermionic
and gauge field in which gauge field interact with fermionic field through the dimensionless coupling parameter
e
. This interaction is represented
in Fig.
25
. Using this interaction
in perturbative techniques, one can compute Feynman diagrams for the theory.

\bullet

The second and third term together will lead to the equation of motion for gauge field
\partial_{\mu}F^{\mu\nu}=-j^{\nu}
.

\bullet

If one checks the gauge invariance of second and third term together, then it will lead to a conserved current density and
the Lagrangian density will then differ by a total derivative, but leaves the equation of motion unchanged .

9.4
Gauge Fixing

One of the problem of (
396
) is that it can not be quantised. One way to see this is that the propagator for photon does not exist.
As we have already experienced from scalar and fermionic fields that the propagator is obtained from free theory,
we start with the free Lagrangian density for gauge field (photon)

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}F_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu}=-\frac{1}{4}\left[\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}\right]\left[\partial^{\mu}A^{\nu}-\partial^{\nu}A^{\mu}\right]

(397)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}\left[\partial^{\mu}A^{\nu}-\partial^{\nu}A^{\mu}\right]+\partial_{\mu}(\cdots)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}A_{\nu}\left[\eta^{\mu\nu}\Box-\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\right]A_{\mu},

where we have interchanged
\mu\rightarrow\nu
in the second term inside the first square braces in the second line. We have done the
integration by parts to arrive at third line. Now using the Fourier decomposition of the gauge field
one can get

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}A_{\nu}\left[-\eta^{\mu\nu}P^{2}+P^{\mu}P^{\nu}\right]A^{\mu},

(398)

where the quantity inside the square braces should in principle be the inverse of photon propagator say
(D_{0}^{\mu\nu})^{-1}
as

\displaystyle(D_{0}^{\mu\nu})^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\eta^{\mu\nu}P^{2}+P^{\mu}P^{\nu}.

(399)

The inverse of
(D_{0}^{\mu\nu})^{-1}
should give the photon propagator as

\displaystyle D_{0}^{\mu\nu}=a\eta^{\mu\nu}+bP^{\mu}P^{\nu}.

(400)

Now

\displaystyle D_{0}^{\mu\lambda}(D_{0})^{-1}_{\lambda\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta^{\mu}_{\nu}

\displaystyle\left(a\eta^{\mu\lambda}+bP^{\mu}P^{\lambda}\right)\left(-\eta_{\lambda\nu}P^{2}+P_{\lambda}P_{\nu}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta^{\mu}_{\nu}

\displaystyle-aP^{2}\delta^{\mu}_{\nu}+aP^{\mu}P_{\nu}-bP^{2}P^{\mu}P_{\nu}+bP^{2}P^{\mu}P^{\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta^{\mu}_{\nu}

\displaystyle-aP^{2}\delta^{\mu}_{\nu}+aP^{\mu}P_{\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta^{\mu}_{\nu}.

(401)

Comparing the coefficients in both sides, one gets
a=-1/P^{2}
and
a=0
but
b
remains undetermined. The matrix
(D^{\mu\nu}_{0})^{-1}

is not invertible, so the propagator
D^{\mu\nu}_{0}
does not exist.

[
Another way: In general any second rank tensor can be written as

{\cal M}^{\mu\nu}=c{\cal P}^{\mu\nu}_{L}+d{\cal P}^{\mu\nu}_{T},

(402)

where the longitudinal and transverse projection operator in orthogonal subspace are

\displaystyle{\cal P}_{L}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P^{\mu}P^{\nu}/P^{2},

\displaystyle{\cal P}_{T}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\eta^{\mu\nu}-{P^{\mu}P^{\nu}}/{P^{2}}\right),

(403)

which satisfy the properties of the projection operator as

{\cal P}_{L}^{\mu\nu}+{\cal P}_{T}^{\mu\nu}=\eta^{\mu\nu},\,\,\,{\cal P}_{L}^{2}={\cal P}_{L},\,\,\,{\cal P}_{T}^{2}={\cal P}_{T},\,\,\,{\cal P}_{L}{\cal P}_{T}=0

(404)

The inverse of (
402
) can be written as

({\cal M}^{\mu\nu})^{-1}=c^{-1}{\cal P}^{\mu\nu}_{L}+d^{-1}{\cal P}^{\mu\nu}_{T}.

(405)

Now (
399
) can be written in terms of projection operator
as

\displaystyle(D^{\mu\nu})^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\eta^{\mu\nu}P^{2}+P^{\mu}P^{\nu}=(0){\cal P}_{L}^{\mu\nu}+(-P^{2}){\cal P}_{T}^{\mu\nu}.

(406)

Now comparing (
406
) with (
402
) one gets
c=0
and
d=-P^{2}
. Therefore,
(
405
) suggests that
d^{-1}=-1/P^{2}
and
c^{-1}
does not exist as
c^{-1}=1/0
.
Thus the inverse of (
406
) also does not exist.

]

Some of the reasons are noted below:

\bullet

The momenta conjugate to the
A^{\mu}
are given by

\displaystyle\pi^{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\delta{\cal L}_{em}}{\delta{\dot{A}}_{\mu}}=\frac{\delta{\cal L}_{em}}{\delta(\partial_{0}A_{\mu})}=F^{\mu 0}\,

(407)

which give
\pi^{0}=0
, as the diagonal components of
F^{\mu\nu}
are zero. This clearly indicates that one of the canonical
momenta does not exist. The equations defining canonical momenta cannot therefore be inverted to express quantities
\dot{A}_{\mu}

in terms of the momenta. So, the Hamiltonian formalism also does not exist.

\bullet

In Maxwell’s equations there are six field variables from both
\mathbf{E}
and
\mathbf{B}
but the two
homogeneous equations imply four constraints on the electromagnetic field components so that there only are two independent
component of electromagnetic fields. Therefore, all components of the gauge field
A^{\mu}
are not independent but they are
connected by gauge transformation, even though only two components are independent.

\bullet

The matrix in the transverse projection operator in (
399
) has zero eigenvalue as can be seen

\displaystyle\left(-\eta^{\mu\nu}P^{2}+P^{\mu}P^{\nu}\right)P_{\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\left(\eta^{\mu\nu}-\frac{P^{\mu}P^{\nu}}{P^{2}}\right)P_{\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle{\cal P}_{T}^{\mu\nu}P_{\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.

(408)

The (
408
) is the transversality condition that projects on to orthogonal subspace. This also indicates that
the presence of zero eigenvalues of the
{\cal P}_{T}^{\mu\nu}
is a direct consequence of the gauge invariance of the theory.
Gauge invariance implies that the theory contains fewer degrees of freedom (only transverse degrees of freedom),
which reflects itself the presence of zero eigenvalues in the quadratic part of the Lagrangian
density in (
395
) vis-a-vis (
397
). However, gauge field
A^{\mu}
is represented by four components
A^{\mu}
,
all of which are not independent but connected by gauge transformation. There are only two independent components and one
needs to eliminate or restrict the additional degrees of gauge freedom. It is also to be noted that the degrees of gauge
freedom absent in (
395
) should not reappear through the interaction. That is ensured through the interaction of the
gauge field with a conserved fermionic current as noted in the last item after (
396
) in previous page.

Before quantisation this redundancy is dealt by fixing the gauge that restrict the gauge degrees
of freedom in the theory. The Lagrangian density for the gauge field (photon) in
U(1)
gauge theory can be
rewritten along with an addition term (referred as gauge fixing term with a gauge parameter
\xi
) as

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}F_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{2\xi}(\partial_{\nu}A^{\nu})(\partial_{\mu}A^{\mu})

(409)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\left[\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}\right]\left[\partial^{\mu}A^{\nu}-\partial^{\nu}A^{\mu}\right]-\frac{1}{2\xi}(\partial_{\nu}A^{\nu})(\partial_{\mu}A^{\mu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}\left[\partial^{\mu}A^{\nu}-\partial^{\nu}A^{\mu}\right]-\frac{1}{2\xi}(\partial_{\nu}A^{\nu})(\partial_{\mu}A^{\mu})+\partial_{\mu}(\cdots)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}A_{\nu}\left[\eta^{\mu\nu}\Box-\left(1-\frac{1}{\xi}\right)\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\right]A_{\mu},

where the inverse of the propagator can be written as

\displaystyle(D_{0}^{\mu\nu})^{-1}=\left[-\eta^{\mu\nu}P^{2}+\left(1-\frac{1}{\xi}\right)P^{\mu}P^{\nu}\right],

(410)

and the inverse of which should give us the correct form the propagator as

\displaystyle D_{0}^{\mu\nu}=\left[a\eta^{\mu\nu}+b\left(1-\frac{1}{\xi}\right)P^{\mu}P^{\nu}\right]\,.

(411)

As before solving for coefficients
a
and
b
:

\displaystyle D_{0}^{\mu\lambda}(D_{0})^{-1}_{\lambda\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta^{\mu}_{\nu}

\displaystyle\left[a\eta^{\mu\lambda}+b\left(1-\frac{1}{\xi}\right)P^{\mu}P^{\lambda}\right]\left[-\eta_{\lambda\nu}P^{2}+\left(1-\frac{1}{\xi}\right)P_{\lambda}P_{\nu}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta^{\mu}_{\nu}

\displaystyle-aP^{2}\delta^{\mu}_{\nu}+\left(1-\frac{1}{\xi}\right)\left[a-bP^{2}+b\left(1-\frac{1}{\xi}\right)P^{2}\right]P^{\mu}P_{\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta^{\mu}_{\nu},

(412)

where comparing the coefficients, one gets

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{P^{2}},

\displaystyle\left[a-bP^{2}+b\left(1-\frac{1}{\xi}\right)P^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\,\,\,b

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\xi}{P^{4}}.

(413)

With these the photon propagator from (
411
) reads as

\displaystyle D_{0}^{\mu\nu}=-\frac{1}{P^{2}}\left[\eta^{\mu\nu}-\left(1-\xi\right)\frac{P^{\mu}P^{\nu}}{P^{2}}\right].

(414)

The Feynman propagator will read as

\displaystyle{\cal D}_{0}^{\mu\nu}=iD_{0}^{\mu\nu}=-\frac{i}{P^{2}}\left[\eta^{\mu\nu}-\left(1-\xi\right)\frac{P^{\mu}P^{\nu}}{P^{2}}\right].

(415)

It is worth noting at this point if a gauge field has mass then the gauge fixing is not required, as we will see later
when the massive vector boson discussed in subsec
8.5
. In different gauges
\xi
takes different values as
\xi=1
(Feynman gauge) and

\xi=0
(Landau gauge). However, the final result is independent of gauge choice, so one can choose it conveniently for the purpose.

After gauge fixing the QED Lagrangian reads as

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{em}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}F_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{2\xi}(\partial_{\mu}A^{\mu})^{2}+{\bar{\psi}}\left(i\gamma^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}-m\right)\psi-e\bar{\psi}\gamma^{\mu}\psi A_{\mu}.

(416)

Now one can show that this gauge fixing term does not change the Lagrangian or the Maxwell’s equations as long as the current is
conserved:
The new equation of motion becomes

\displaystyle\Box A^{\nu}-\left(1-\frac{1}{\xi}\right)\partial^{\nu}\left(\partial_{\mu}A^{\mu}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-j^{\nu}.

(417)

Operating 4-divergence and using the current conservation
\partial_{\mu}j^{\mu}=0
one gets

\displaystyle\Box\left(\partial_{\mu}A^{\mu}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\partial_{\mu}j^{\mu}=0

\displaystyle\rightarrow\,\,\,\partial_{\mu}A^{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,

(418)

with appropriate boundary condition on
A^{\mu}
so that (
418
) is satisfied. This implies that one can always transform
A^{\mu}

according to (
391
) so that it satisfies (
418
). This is called gauge fixing condition in covariant gauge.
Now one can compute canonical momenta and do the Hamiltonian formulation.

9.5
Free Photon Partition Function

The free photon Lagrangian density can be written from (
416
) as

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}F_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu}.

(419)

In vacuum, photon partition function can be written as,

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\mathcal{D}A_{\mu}\exp{[iS]}

(420)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\mathcal{D}A_{\mu}\exp{\left[{i\int d^{4}X\left(-\frac{1}{4}F_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu}\right)}\right]}\,,

where
{\cal D}A_{\mu}={\cal D}A_{0}{\cal D}A_{1}{\cal D}A_{2}{\cal D}A_{3}
.
Gauge transformations should not change anything physically.

We now choose a covariant gauge condition as

G(A)=\partial_{\mu}A^{\mu}=w(X),

(421)

which can be imposed to (
420
) by inserting a identity
Yang
given as

\int\mathcal{D}\alpha(x)\delta\left(G(A,\alpha)-w(X)\right)\Big|{\frac{\delta\left(G(A,\alpha)-w(X)\right)}{\delta\alpha}}\Big|=1\,.

(422)

Lorentz gauge condition can be recovered by taking
w=0
. There is still a residual gauge freedom as one shifts the gauge field

A_{\mu}\rightarrow A_{\mu}+\partial_{\mu}\alpha(X)\,,

(423)

where the phase factor,
\alpha(X)
is differentiable function of space-time.
Now we can write the gauge condition in (
421
) as

G(A,\alpha)=\partial_{\mu}A^{\mu}+\partial_{\mu}\partial^{\mu}\alpha\,.

(424)

The determinant term can be obtained as

\frac{\delta\left(G(A,\alpha(X))-w(X)\right)}{\delta\alpha(Y)}=\partial_{\mu}\partial^{\mu}\delta^{(4)}(X-Y)\,.

(425)

So (
420
) becomes,

\mathcal{Z}=\int\mathcal{D}A_{\mu}\mathcal{D}\alpha(X)\delta\left(G(A,\alpha)-w(X)\right)\Big|{\frac{\delta\left(G(A,\alpha)-w(X)\right)}{\delta\alpha}}\Big|\exp{\left[{i\int d^{4}X\left(-\frac{1}{4}F_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu}\right)}\right]}\,.

(426)

Since there is a residual gauge freedom, we shift the gauge field as (
423
)
and write (
426
) as

\mathcal{Z}=\int\mathcal{D}A_{\mu}\,\,\mathcal{D}\alpha(X)\,\,\delta\left(G(A)-w(X)\right)\,\det{\partial^{2}}\exp{\left[{i\int d^{4}X\left(-\frac{1}{4}F_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu}\right)}\right]}\,.

(427)

Now the integrand does not contain
\alpha
and the
\alpha
integration gives diverging result.
This is due to the redundancy of the residual gauge transformation. Now,

w(X)
is an arbitrary function of
X
and the behaviour of
w(X)
is not known, so
the integral involved in the partition function can not be solved.
One can avoid this problem by averaging over
w(X)
around zero with a Gaussian width
\xi

\int\mathcal{D}w\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi\xi}}\exp{\frac{w^{2}(X)}{2\xi}}\,,

(428)

where
\xi
is a gauge fixing parameter that one chooses for the convenience of calculations and
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi\xi}}
is normalisation factor.
Now the integration over
w
is performed in (
427
) and one gets

\mathcal{Z}=\int\mathcal{D}A_{\mu}\,\,\det{\partial^{2}}\exp{\left[i\int d^{4}X\left(-\frac{1}{4}F_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu}-\frac{(\partial_{\mu}A^{\mu})^{2}}{2\xi}\right)\right]}\,.

(429)

Now we can write
\det{\partial^{2}}
term using Grassmann property given in (
81
) as

\det{\partial^{2}}=\int\mathcal{D}\bar{C}\,\,\mathcal{D}C\,\exp({-\bar{C}\partial^{2}C})\,,

(430)

where
C
is Grassmann field which is also known as ghost field.
This plays a crucial role to cancel the redundant gauge degrees of freedom.
Now,

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\mathcal{D}A\,\,\mathcal{D}\bar{C}\,\,\mathcal{D}C\,\exp{\left[i\int d^{4}X(\mathcal{L}_{\gamma}+\mathcal{L}_{\textrm{gf}}+\mathcal{L}_{\textrm{gh})}\right]}

(431)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\gamma+\textrm{gf}}\mathcal{Z}_{\textrm{gh}}\,,

where gauge fixing and ghost terms, respectively, are

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\textrm{gf}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{(\partial_{\mu}A^{\mu})^{2}}{2\xi}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\textrm{gh}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\bar{C}\,\partial^{2}C\,.

Let us calculate the contributions of gauge and gauge fixing part of (
431
) to the partition function as

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\gamma+\textrm{gf}}

\displaystyle{=\atop{t\rightarrow-i\tau}}

\displaystyle\int\mathcal{D}A\exp{\left(\frac{i}{2}\int d^{4}XA_{\nu}\left[\eta^{\mu\nu}\Box-(1-\frac{1}{\xi})\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}\right]A_{\mu}\right)}\,

(432)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\mathcal{D}A\exp{\left(-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau\,d^{3}\bm{\vec{x}}\,A_{i}\left[\delta^{ij}\Box_{\tau}+(1-\frac{1}{\xi})\partial^{i}\partial^{j}\right]A_{j}\right)}\,,

where we have used
\eta^{\mu\nu}\leftrightarrow-\delta^{ij}
.

The Fourier transform of the gauge field is given as

\displaystyle A_{i}(X)=A_{i}(\bm{\vec{x}},\tau)

\displaystyle{=\atop{t\rightarrow-i\tau}}\atop{p_{0}\rightarrow i\omega_{n}}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{V\beta}}\sum_{P}\,e^{-iP\cdot X}\,A_{i}(P)

(433)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{V\beta}}\sum_{n,\bm{\vec{p}}}\,e^{i\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\vec{x}}}\,\,e^{-i\omega_{n}\tau}\,A_{i}(\omega_{n},\bm{\vec{p}})\,.

One can write the partition function in (
432
) in frequency momentum space as

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\gamma+\textrm{gf}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\mathcal{D}A(\omega_{n},\bm{\vec{p}})\exp{\left(-\sum_{n,\bm{\vec{p}}}\frac{1}{2}A^{*}_{i}(\omega_{n},\bm{\vec{p}})\left[\delta^{ij}(\omega_{n}^{2}+p^{2})-(1-\frac{1}{\xi})p^{i}p^{j}\right]A_{j}(\omega_{n},\bm{\vec{p}})\right)}

(434)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\mathcal{D}A(\omega_{n},\bm{\vec{p}})\exp{\left(-\sum_{n,\bm{\vec{p}}}\frac{1}{2}A^{*}_{i}(\omega_{n},\bm{\vec{p}})D^{-1}_{ij}A_{j}(\omega_{n},\bm{\vec{p}})\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{n,\bm{\vec{p}}}\sqrt{\frac{\pi^{D}}{\det{D^{-1}_{ij}(\xi)}}}

where
D^{-1}_{ij}
is a
4\times 4
matrix and inverse of the gauge boson propagator. Let us set
p=(0,0,p)
as three space part of
p
are in equal footing now
Yang
.
In Feynman gauge we have
\xi=1
.

D^{-1}_{ij}=\left({\begin{array}[]{cccc}p^{2}+\omega_{n}^{2}&0&0&0\\
0&p^{2}+\omega_{n}^{2}&0&0\\
0&0&p^{2}+\omega_{n}^{2}&0\\
0&0&0&p^{2}+\omega_{n}^{2}\\
\end{array}}\right)

\therefore\det{D^{-1}}_{ij}=(p^{2}+\omega_{n}^{2})^{4}.

(435)

\displaystyle\mbox{So,}\,\,\,\ln{\mathcal{Z}_{\gamma+\textrm{gf}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\sum\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\int\limits_{n,\bm{\vec{p}}}\ln\textrm{det}[D_{ij}^{-1}]

(436)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-4\times\frac{1}{2}\sum\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\int\limits_{P_{E}}\ln{[P_{E}^{2}]}\,,

where
P_{E}
is Euclidean momentum and
\sum\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\int\limits_{P_{E}}
is a bosonic sum-integral.

Now we have to calculate ghost contribution to the partition function from (
431
) as

{\mathcal{Z}}_{\textrm{ gh}}=\int\mathcal{D}{\bar{C}}\,\,\mathcal{D}C\,\exp{\left(-i\int d^{4}X\,\bar{C}\,\partial^{2}\,C\right)}=\int\mathcal{D}{\bar{C}}\,\,\mathcal{D}C\,\,\exp{\left(\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau\,d^{3}x\,\bar{C}\,\Box_{\tau}\,C\right)}\,.

(437)

The Fourier transform of the ghost field is given as

\displaystyle C(\tau,\bm{\vec{x}})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{V\beta}}\sum_{n,\bm{\vec{p}}}\,e^{i\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\vec{x}}}\,e^{-i\omega_{n}\tau}\,C(\omega_{n},\bm{\vec{p}})\,.

(438)

Combining (
437
) and (
438
), one can get the ghost partition function in
frequency momentum space as

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Z}}_{\textrm{ gh}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\mathcal{D}{\bar{C}(\omega_{n},\bm{\vec{p}})}\mathcal{D}C(\omega_{n},\bm{\vec{p}})\exp{\left(-\sum_{n,\bm{\vec{p}}}\bar{C}(\omega_{n},\bm{\vec{p}})(\omega_{n}^{2}+p^{2})C(\omega_{n},\bm{\vec{p}})\right)}

(439)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{n,\bm{\vec{p}}}(\omega_{n}^{2}+p^{2}).

\mbox{So,}\,\,\,\ln{\mathcal{Z}_{\textrm{gh}}}=2\times\frac{1}{2}\sum\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\int\limits_{P_{E}}\ln{[P_{E}^{2}]}\,.

(440)

The logarithm of the photon partition function can be written as

\displaystyle\ln{\mathcal{Z}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ln{\mathcal{Z}}_{\gamma+\textrm{gf}}+\ln{\mathcal{Z}}_{\textrm{gh}}

(441)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-4\times\frac{1}{2}\sum\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\int\limits_{P_{E}}\ln{[P_{E}^{2}]}+2\times\frac{1}{2}\sum\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\int\limits_{P_{E}}\ln{[P_{E}^{2}]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\int\limits_{P_{E}}\ln{[P_{E}^{2}]}\,.

So, ghost contribution cancels two unphysical degrees of freedom of photon. Now there are two physical transverse degrees of freedom of photon.

By calculating the frequency sum one gets,

\ln{\mathcal{Z}}=-2V\int\frac{d^{3}p}{(2\pi)^{3}}\left[\frac{\beta\omega_{p}}{2}+\ln{(1-\exp{(-\beta\omega_{p})})}\right]\,.

(442)

which agrees with one bosonic degree of freedom in (
16
).

So free energy density of non-interacting photon at finite temperature is given by (ignoring vacuum part)

\displaystyle F_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{T}{V}\ln{\mathcal{Z}}=2\int\frac{d^{3}p}{(2\pi)^{3}}\ln{(1-\exp{(-\beta\omega_{p})})}

(443)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\pi^{2}T^{4}}{45}\,.

The pressure for non-interacting photon

{\cal P}_{0}=-F_{0}=\frac{\pi^{2}T^{4}}{45}\,.

(444)

9.6
One-loop Fermion Self-energy
\Sigma
and Structure Functions
{\cal A}
and
{\cal B}
in HTL approximation

We have electron-photon interaction Lagrangian from (
416
) as

\mathcal{L}_{int}=-e\bar{\psi}\gamma_{\mu}\psi A^{\mu}.

(445)

Figure 26:
One loop fermion self-energy diagram.

The one loop fermion self-energy in Fig.
26
can be written in Feynman gauge as

\displaystyle\Sigma(P)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x^{2}C_{F}T\sum\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\int\limits_{\{K\}}\gamma_{\mu}\frac{K\!\!\!\!/\penalty}{K^{2}}\gamma^{\mu}\frac{1}{(P-K)^{2}}

(446)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2x^{2}C_{F}T\sum\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\int\limits_{\{K\}}\frac{K\!\!\!\!/\penalty}{K^{2}\ Q^{2}}\,,

where
Q=(P-K)
and
\sum\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\int\limits_{\{K\}}
is a fermionic sum-integral. Also
x
and
C_{F}
are, respectively, coupling and Casimir invariant for a given group. For
U(1)
group
x=e
and
C_{F}=1
and Eq.(
446
) corresponds to electron self-energy.
For
SU(3)
group
x=g
and
C_{F}=4/3
and Eq.(
446
) corresponds to quark self-energy with internal photon line should be replaced by gluon line.

We now would like to compute the structure functions as given in the rest frame of the heat bath, respectively, in (
310
) and (
312
) as

\displaystyle{\cal A}(\omega,p)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4p^{2}}\left({\rm{Tr}}\left[\Sigma P\!\!\!\!/\penalty\,\right]-\omega{\rm{Tr}}\left[\Sigma u\!\!\!/\penalty\right]\right),

(447a)

\displaystyle{\cal B}(\omega,p)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4p^{2}}\left(P^{2}{\rm{Tr}}\left[\Sigma u\!\!\!/\penalty\right]-(P\cdot u){\rm{Tr}}\left[\Sigma P\!\!\!\!/\penalty\,\right]\right).

(447b)

Now, we can write

\displaystyle{\textrm{Tr}}\left[\Sigma P\!\!\!\!/\penalty\,\right]

\displaystyle\,=\,-8x^{2}C_{F}T\sum\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\int\limits_{\{K\}}\frac{k_{0}\omega-\bm{{\vec{k}}\cdot{\vec{p}}}}{K^{2}Q^{2}}\,,

(448a)

\displaystyle{\textrm{Tr}}\left[\Sigma u\!\!\!/\penalty\right]

\displaystyle\,=\ -8x^{2}C_{F}T\sum\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\int\limits_{\{K\}}\frac{k_{0}}{K^{2}Q^{2}}\,.

(448b)

Using (
448a
) and (
448b
) in (
447a
), we get

{\cal A}(\omega,p)=\frac{2x^{2}C_{F}}{p^{2}}T\sum\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\int\limits_{\{K\}}\frac{\bm{{\vec{k}}\cdot{\vec{p}}}}{K^{2}Q^{2}}\,.

(449)

We use the following frequency sum in mixed representation

\displaystyle T\sum_{k_{0}}\frac{1}{K^{2}Q^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4kq}\left[\left(1-n_{F}(k)+n_{B}(q)\right)\left(\frac{1}{\omega+k+q}-\frac{1}{\omega-k-q}\right)\right.

(450)

\displaystyle\left.+\left(n_{B}(q)+n_{F}(k)\right)\left(\frac{1}{\omega+k-q}-\frac{1}{\omega-k+q}\right)\right]\,,

where
n_{F\atop B}(y)=(e^{y/T}\pm 1)^{-1}
.
When loop momentum is hard,
K\sim T
, compare to the external momentum
P
is called hard thermal loop (HTL) approximation
BP
.
Under this HTL approximation
6
6

6

The details of HTL approximation will be discussed in section
12
.
one can make following simplifications as

\displaystyle q\,=\,\left|\bm{\vec{p}}-\bm{\vec{k}}\right|

\displaystyle\,=\,\sqrt{p^{2}+k^{2}-2pk\cos\theta}=\sqrt{p^{2}+k^{2}-2pkc}\approx k-pc=k-{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}\,,

(451a)

\displaystyle n_{B}(q)

\displaystyle\,=n_{B}(k-{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}})\approx n_{B}(k)-{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}\frac{dn_{B}(k)}{dk}\,,

(451b)

\displaystyle\omega\pm k\pm q

\displaystyle\,=\omega\pm k\pm k\mp\,{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}\approx\pm 2k\,,

(451c)

\displaystyle\omega\pm k\mp q

\displaystyle\,=\omega\pm k\mp k\pm\,{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}\approx\omega\pm{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}\ .

(451d)

Using (
451a
) to (
451d
) in (
450
), one can write

\displaystyle T\sum_{k_{0}}\frac{1}{K^{2}Q^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4k^{2}}\left[\left(1-n_{F}(k)+n_{B}(k)-{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}\frac{dn_{B}(k)}{dk}\right)\left(\frac{1}{k}\right)\right.

(452)

\displaystyle\left.+\left(n_{B}(k)+n_{F}(k)-{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}\frac{dn_{B}(k)}{dk}\right)\left(\frac{1}{\omega+{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}}-\frac{1}{\omega-{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}}\right)\right]\,,

Combining (
452
) and (
449
), we get

\displaystyle{\cal A}(\omega,p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2x^{2}C_{F}}{p^{2}}\int\frac{d^{3}k}{(2\pi)^{3}}\frac{\bm{{\vec{p}}\cdot{\vec{k}}}}{4k^{2}}\left[\left(1-n_{F}(k)+n_{B}(k)-{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}\frac{dn_{B}(k)}{dk}\right)\left(\frac{1}{k}\right)\right.

(453)

\displaystyle\left.+\left(n_{B}(k)+n_{F}(k)-{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}\frac{dn_{B}(k)}{dk}\right)\left(\frac{1}{\omega+{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}}-\frac{1}{\omega-{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}}\right)\right]\,.

We note that the term
1/k
inside the square bracket contributes as proportional to
T
, which is sub-leading in
T
. This term can be neglected. We now
evaluate the second term only that contributes as
T^{2}
, which is leading order in
T
. We further note that the term

{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}\frac{dn_{B}(k)}{dk}
has soft momentum and is neglected. With this the (
453
) reduces as

\displaystyle{\cal A}(\omega,p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2x^{2}C_{F}}{4p^{2}}\int\frac{k\,dk\,d\Omega}{(2\pi)^{3}}\left(n_{B}(k)+n_{F}(k)\right)\left[\frac{{\bm{{\vec{p}}\cdot{\hat{k}}}}}{\omega+{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}}-\frac{{\bm{{\vec{p}}\cdot{\hat{k}}}}}{\omega-{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}}\right]\,.

(454)

Now putting
\cos\theta\rightarrow-\cos\theta
in the first term inside the square bracket, the above equation then becomes

\displaystyle{\cal A}(\omega,p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{x^{2}C_{F}}{p^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}k\,dk\left(n_{B}(k)+n_{F}(k)\right)\ \int\frac{d\Omega}{(2\pi)^{3}}\frac{{\bm{{\vec{p}}\cdot{\hat{k}}}}}{\omega-{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}}\,.

(455)

We can perform the
k
-integration as

\int_{0}^{\infty}k\,dk\left(n_{B}(k)+n_{F}(k)\right)=\frac{\pi^{2}T^{2}}{4}.

(456)

Using (
456
) we can obtain

\displaystyle{\cal A}(\omega,p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{m^{2}_{\textrm{th}}}{p^{2}}\int\frac{d\Omega}{4\pi}\frac{{\bm{{\vec{p}}\cdot{\hat{k}}}}}{\omega-{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}}

(457)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{m^{2}_{\textrm{th}}}{p^{2}}\int\frac{d\Omega}{4\pi}\frac{{\bm{{\vec{p}}\cdot{\hat{k}}}}}{P\cdot\hat{K}}\,,

where
{\hat{K}}=(1,\bm{\hat{k}})
is a light like vector and the thermal mass of the fermion is given as

\displaystyle m_{\textrm{th}}^{2}=\frac{x^{2}C_{F}T^{2}}{8}\,.

(458)

For electron the thermal mass becomes
m_{\textrm{th}}^{2}=\frac{e^{2}T^{2}}{8}
whereas for quark
m_{\textrm{th}}^{2}=\frac{g^{2}T^{2}}{6}
.

The angular integration can be performed using the following integrals

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{dy}{a-by}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{b}\ln\frac{a+b}{a-b}\,,

(459a)

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{y\ dy}{a-by}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2}{b}+\frac{a}{b^{2}}\ln\frac{a+b}{a-b}.

(459b)

After performing the angular integration in (
457
) we finally get
HAW
;
VVK

\mathcal{A}(\omega,p)\,=\,\frac{m_{\textrm{th}}^{2}}{p^{2}}\left[1-\frac{\omega}{2p}\ln\left(\frac{\omega+p}{\omega-p}\right)\right]\,,\\

(460)

Using (
448a
) and (
448b
) in (
447b
) we write the structure function
{\cal B}(\omega,p)
as

\displaystyle{\cal B}(\omega,p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2x^{2}C_{F}}{p^{2}}\left[p^{2}T\sum\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\int\limits_{\{K\}}\frac{k_{0}}{K^{2}Q^{2}}-(P\cdot u)T\sum\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\int\limits_{\{K\}}\frac{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\vec{k}}}{K^{2}Q^{2}}\right]\,.

(461)

The frequency sum in the second term inside square bracket is already done in (
450
) . The frequency sum needed for the first term is given in mixed representation as

\displaystyle T\sum_{k_{0}}\frac{k_{0}}{K^{2}Q^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4q}\left[\left(1-n_{F}(k)+n_{B}(q)\right)\left(\frac{1}{\omega+k+q}+\frac{1}{\omega-k-q}\right)\right.

(462)

\displaystyle\left.+\left(n_{B}(q)+n_{F}(k)\right)\left(\frac{1}{\omega+k-q}+\frac{1}{\omega-k+q}\right)\right]\,,

Using HTL approximations in (
451a
) to (
451d
), the frequency sum becomes

\displaystyle T\sum_{k_{0}}\frac{k_{0}}{K^{2}Q^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4k}\left[\left(n_{B}(k)+n_{F}(k)-{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}\frac{dn_{B}(k)}{dk}\right)\left(\frac{1}{\omega+\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}+\frac{1}{\omega-\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}\right)\right]\,,

(463)

Substituting (
452
) and (
463
) in (
461
), we get

\displaystyle{\cal B}(\omega,p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2x^{2}C_{F}}{p^{2}}\left[-p^{2}\int\frac{d^{3}k}{(2\pi)^{3}}\frac{1}{4k}\left(n_{B}(k)+n_{F}(k)-{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}\frac{dn_{B}(k)}{dk}\right)\left(\frac{1}{\omega+\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}+\frac{1}{\omega-\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}\right)\right.

(464)

\displaystyle\left.-(P\cdot u)\int\frac{d^{3}k}{(2\pi)^{3}}\frac{\bm{\vec{p}}\cdot\bm{\vec{k}}}{4k^{2}}\left(1-n_{F}(k)+n_{B}(k)-{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}\frac{dn_{B}(k)}{dk}\right)\frac{1}{k}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-(P\cdot u)\int\frac{d^{3}k}{(2\pi)^{3}}\frac{1}{4k^{2}}\left(n_{B}(k)+n_{F}(k)-{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}\frac{dn_{B}(k)}{dk}\right)\left(\frac{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\vec{k}}}{\omega+\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}-\frac{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\vec{k}}}{\omega-\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}\right)\right]

As before we neglect the second term inside the square bracket that gives a contribution proportional to
T
, which is sub-leading in
T
. Also

{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}\frac{dn_{B}(k)}{dk}
term is neglected as the soft momentum is associated with it. Then, the above equation can be written as

\displaystyle{\cal B}(\omega,p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{x^{2}C_{F}}{p^{2}}\left[\int_{0}^{\infty}k\ dk\left(n_{B}(k)+n_{F}(k)\right)\int\frac{d\Omega}{(2\pi)^{3}}\frac{(P\cdot u)(\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}})-p^{2}}{\omega-\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}\right]\,.

(465)

After performing the
k
-integration using (
456
), we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}(\omega,p)

\displaystyle\,=\,

\displaystyle\frac{m_{\textrm{th}}^{2}}{p^{2}}\int\frac{d\Omega}{4\pi}\frac{(P\cdot u)(\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}})-p^{2}}{P\cdot\hat{K}}\,,

(466)

Now performing the angular integrations using (
459a
) and (
459b
), we finally get
HAW
;
VVK

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}(\omega,p)

\displaystyle\,=\,

\displaystyle\frac{m_{\textrm{th}}^{2}}{p}\left[-\frac{\omega}{p}+\left(\frac{\omega^{2}}{p^{2}}-1\right)\frac{1}{2}\ln\left(\frac{\omega+p}{\omega-p}\right)\right]\,.

(467)

The fermion self-energy can be written from (
446
) as

\displaystyle\Sigma(P)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2x^{2}C_{F}T\sum\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\int\limits_{K}\left[\frac{k_{0}\gamma_{0}}{K^{2}Q^{2}}-\frac{\vec{\gamma}\cdot\bm{\vec{k}}}{K^{2}Q^{2}}\right]\,.

(468)

Following the same procedures as those structure functions, the fermion self-energy contribution within HTL approximation can be written as

\displaystyle\Sigma(P)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x^{2}C_{F}\int_{0}^{\infty}k\ dk\left(n_{B}(k)+n_{F}(k)\right)\int\frac{d\Omega}{(2\pi)^{3}}\left[\frac{\gamma_{0}}{\omega-\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}-\frac{\vec{\gamma}\cdot\bm{\hat{k}}}{\omega-\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}\right]\,,

(469)

which after
k
-integration becomes

\displaystyle\Sigma(P)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m_{\textrm{th}}^{2}\int\frac{d\Omega}{4\pi}\frac{\hat{\not{K}}}{P\cdot\hat{K}}.

(470)

After explicit calculations, we obtain

\displaystyle\Sigma(P)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{m_{\textrm{th}}^{2}}{2p}\ln\left(\frac{\omega+p}{\omega-p}\right)\gamma_{0}+\frac{m_{\textrm{th}}^{2}}{p}\left[1-\frac{\omega}{2p}\ln\left(\frac{\omega+p}{\omega-p}\right)\right]\left({\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}}\right)\,.

(471)

This expression can also be obtained directly by combining (
313
), (
460
) and (
467
).

9.7
Dispersion of Fermionic Quasiparticles and Collective Excitations in HTL Approximation

Figure 27:
Plot of quasiparticles dispersion in HTL approximation.

We can obtain
{\cal D}_{\pm}(\omega,p)
by combining (
320
), (
460
) and (
467
) as

\displaystyle{\cal D}_{\pm}(\omega,p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[1+\frac{m_{\textrm{th}}^{2}}{p^{2}}\left(1-\frac{\omega}{2p}\ln\left(\frac{\omega+p}{\omega-p}\right)\right)\right](\omega\mp p)+\frac{m_{\textrm{th}}^{2}}{p}\left[-\frac{\omega}{p}+\left(\frac{\omega^{2}}{p^{2}}-1\right)\frac{1}{2}\ln\left(\frac{\omega+p}{\omega-p}\right)\right]

(472)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\omega\mp p)-\frac{m_{\textrm{th}}^{2}}{p}\left[\frac{1}{2}\left(1\mp\frac{\omega}{p}\right)\ln\left(\frac{\omega+p}{\omega-p}\right)\pm 1\right]\,.

One can obtain the in-medium fermion propagator reads from (
326
)

S^{\star}(P)=\frac{1}{2}\frac{(\gamma_{0}-{\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}})}{{\cal D}_{+}(\omega,p)}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{(\gamma_{0}+{\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}})}{{\cal D}_{-}(\omega,p)},

(473)

The charge invariance demands that
{\cal D}_{\pm}(-\omega,p)=-{\cal D}_{\mp}(\omega,p)
which
implies that
{\cal A}(-\omega,p)={\cal A}(\omega,p)
and
{\cal B}(-\omega,p)=-{\cal B}(\omega,p)
.
{\cal D}(\omega,p)
has imaginary part for space like
momenta
P\,(p_{0}^{2}<p^{2})
, it is also useful to define the parity properties for both real and imaginary parts of
{\cal D}(\omega,p)
as

{\mathrm{Re}}{\cal D}_{+}(-\omega,p)=-{\mathrm{Re}}{\cal D}_{-}(\omega,p)
and
{\mathrm{Im}}{\cal D}_{+}(-\omega,p)={\mathrm{Im}}{\cal D}_{-}(\omega,p)
.

Although the effective propagator in (
473
) manifests the chiral symmetry, the poles of
{\cal D}_{\pm}(\omega,p)
do not occur at light cone,
\omega=\pm p
. This means that the poles of the effective fermion propagator will be away from the light cone in the time like domain. This is because the extra term
{\cal B}\gamma_{0}
appears in self-energy in (
313
) due to the breaking of Lorentz invariance at finite temperature
HAW
. The zeros of
{\cal D}_{\pm}(\omega,p)
define dispersion property
of a quark in the thermal bath.

{\cal D}_{+}(\omega,p)=0
has two ploes at
\omega=\omega_{+}(p)
and
\omega=-\omega_{-}(p)
whereas

{\cal D}_{-}(\omega,p)=0
has two ploes at
\omega=\omega_{-}(p)
and
\omega=-\omega_{+}(p)
.
Only the positive energy solutions are displayed in Fig.
27
. A mode with energy
\omega_{+}
represents the in-medium propagation of a particle excitation
This is a Dirac spinors and eigenstate of
(\gamma_{0}-{\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}})
with chirality to helicity ratio
+1
.
On the other hand there is a new long wavelength mode known as
plasmino
with energy
\omega_{-}
and eigenstate of

(\gamma_{0}+{\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}})
with chirality to helicity ratio
-1
. The
\omega_{-}
branch has a minimum at low momentum
and then approaches free dispersion curve at large momentum. It is important to note that the minimum leads to Van Hove singularities
in soft dilepton rate
BPY
and meson spectral function
KMT
.

Below we present the approximate analytic solutions of
\omega_{\pm}(p)
for small and large values of momentum
p
.
For small values of momentum (
p<<m_{\textrm{th}}
), the dispersion relations are

\displaystyle\omega_{+}(p)

\displaystyle\,\approx\,m_{\textrm{th}}+\frac{1}{3}p+\frac{1}{3}\frac{p^{2}}{m_{\textrm{th}}}-\frac{16}{135}\frac{p^{3}}{m^{2}_{\textrm{th}}}\,,

(474a)

\displaystyle\omega_{-}(p)

\displaystyle\,\approx\,m_{\textrm{th}}-\frac{1}{3}p+\frac{1}{3}\frac{p^{2}}{m_{\textrm{th}}}+\frac{16}{135}\frac{p^{3}}{m^{2}_{\textrm{th}}}\,,

(474b)

whereas for large values of momentum (
m_{\textrm{th}}<<p<<T
), one obtains

\displaystyle\omega_{+}(p)

\displaystyle\,\approx\,p+\frac{m^{2}_{\textrm{th}}}{p}-\frac{m^{4}_{\textrm{th}}}{2p^{3}}\ln\frac{m^{2}_{\textrm{th}}}{2p^{2}}+\frac{m^{6}_{\textrm{th}}}{4p^{5}}\left[\ln^{2}\frac{m^{2}_{\textrm{th}}}{2p^{2}}+\ln\frac{m^{2}_{\textrm{th}}}{2p^{2}}-1\right]\,,

(475a)

\displaystyle\omega_{-}(p)

\displaystyle\,\approx\,p+2p\exp\left(-\frac{2p^{2}+m^{2}_{\textrm{th}}}{m^{2}_{\textrm{th}}}\right)\,.

(475b)

We note that at large (hard) momentum the collective mode with chirality to helicity ratio
+1
resembles the free particle in vacuum whereas the long wave length mode,
plasmino with chirality to helicity ratio
-1
decouples from the plasma. These are clearly evident from (
475a
) and (
475b
).
At small momenta both collective modes are equally important which could be seen from (
474a
) and (
474b
).
In addition to the pole contributions coming from time like domain (
\omega^{2}>p^{2}
),
{\cal D}_{\pm}(\omega,p)
contains a discontinuous
part corresponding to
Landau damping
coming from space like domain (
\omega^{2}<p^{2}
) due to the presence
of logarithmic term in (
472
).

9.8
Spectral Representation of Fermion Propagator

From (
326
) the in-medium fermion propagator reads as

S^{\star}(P)=\frac{1}{2}\frac{(\gamma_{0}-{\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}})}{{\cal D}_{+}(\omega,p)}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{(\gamma_{0}+{\vec{\gamma}}\cdot\bm{\hat{p}})}{{\cal D}_{-}(\omega,p)},

(476)

where
{\cal D}_{\pm}(\omega,p)
are given in (
472
) as

\displaystyle{\cal D}_{\pm}(\omega,p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p(\frac{\omega}{p}\mp 1)-\frac{m_{\textrm{th}}^{2}}{p}\left[\frac{1}{2}\left(1\mp\frac{\omega}{p}\right)\ln\left(\frac{\frac{\omega}{p}+1}{\frac{\omega}{p}-1}\right)\pm 1\right]\,.

(477)

According to (
321
),
{\cal D}_{\pm}(\omega,p)=d_{\pm}(\omega,p)=\omega\pm p
, for free massless case. The corresponding free spectral function
can be obtained following (
696
) as

\displaystyle\rho^{f}_{\pm}(\omega,k)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\frac{1}{\pi}{\textrm{Im}}\left.\frac{1}{d_{\pm}(\omega,p)}\right|_{\omega\rightarrow\omega+i\epsilon}

(478)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\frac{1}{\pi}{\textrm{Im}}\frac{1}{\omega\mp p+i\epsilon}=\frac{\delta(\omega\mp p)}{\left|\frac{\ d(\omega\mp p)}{d\omega}\right|}=\delta(\omega\mp p)\,.

As discussed in subsec
9.7
that
{\cal D}_{\pm}(\omega,p)
has solutions at
\omega_{\pm}(k)
and
-\omega_{\mp}(p)
and a cut part due to space like momentum ,

\omega^{2}<p^{2}
. In-medium spectral function corresponding to the effective fermion propagator in (
476
) will have both pole and cut contribution as

\rho_{\pm}(\omega,p)=\rho^{\textrm{pole}}_{\pm}(\omega,p)+\rho^{\textrm{cut}}_{\pm}(\omega,p)\,.

(479)

The pole part of the spectral function can be obtained following (
696
) as

\displaystyle\rho^{\textrm{pole}}_{\pm}(\omega,p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\frac{1}{\pi}{\textrm{Im}}\left.\frac{1}{{\cal D}_{\pm}(\omega,p)}\right|_{\omega\rightarrow\omega+i\epsilon}=\frac{\delta(\omega-\omega_{\pm})}{\left|\frac{d{\cal D_{\pm}}}{d\omega}\right|_{\omega=\omega_{\pm}}}+\frac{\delta(\omega+\omega_{\mp})}{\left|\frac{d{\cal D_{\mp}}}{d\omega}\right|_{\omega=-\omega_{\mp}}}\,

(480)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(\omega^{2}-p^{2})}{2m^{2}_{\textrm{th}}}\delta(\omega-\omega_{\pm})+\frac{(\omega^{2}-p^{2})}{2m^{2}_{\textrm{th}}}\delta(\omega+\omega_{\mp})\,.

For
\omega^{2}<p^{2}
, there is a discontinuity in
\ln\frac{\omega+p}{\omega-p}

as
\ln{y}=\ln\left|y\right|-i\pi\ ,
which leads to the spectral
function,
\rho^{\textrm{cut}}_{\pm}(\omega,p)
, corresponding
to the discontinuity in
{\cal D}_{\pm}(\omega,p)
can be obtained from (
694
) as

\displaystyle\rho^{\textrm{cut}}_{\pm}(\omega,p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi i}\textrm{Disc}\frac{1}{{\cal D}_{\pm}(\omega,p)}=\frac{1}{\pi}\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\textrm{Im}\left.\frac{1}{{\cal D}_{\pm}(\omega,p)}\right|_{{\omega\rightarrow\omega+i\epsilon}\atop{\omega<p}}

(481)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\frac{\frac{m^{2}_{\textrm{th}}}{2p}\left(\pm\frac{\omega}{p}-1\right)\Theta(p^{2}-\omega^{2})}{\left[\omega\mp p-\frac{m^{2}_{\textrm{th}}}{p}\left(\pm 1-\frac{p\mp\omega}{2p}\ln\frac{p+\omega}{p-\omega}\right)\right]^{2}+\left[\pi\frac{m^{2}_{\textrm{th}}}{2p}\left(1\mp\frac{\omega}{p}\right)\right]^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta_{\pm}(\omega,p)\Theta(p^{2}-\omega^{2}).

9.9
Calculation of
\Pi_{L}
and
\Pi_{T}
from One-loop Photon Self-energy in HTL Approximation

Figure 28:
One loop photon self-energy diagram.

We have QED interaction Lagrangian from (
416
)

\mathcal{L}_{int}=-e\bar{\psi}\gamma_{\mu}\psi A^{\mu}.

(482)

So using Fig.
28
photon self energy can be written as

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int\frac{d^{4}K}{(2\pi)^{4}}\mathsf{Tr}\left[(-ie\gamma_{\mu})\frac{i\not{K}}{K^{2}}(-ie\gamma_{\nu})\frac{i(\not{P}+\not{K})}{(K+P)^{2}}\right]

(483)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-e^{2}\int\frac{d^{4}K}{(2\pi)^{4}}\mathsf{Tr}{\left[\frac{\gamma_{\mu}\not{K}\gamma_{\nu}(\not{P}+\not{K})}{K^{2}(P+K)^{2}}\right]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-4e^{2}\int\frac{d^{4}K}{(2\pi)^{4}}\left[\frac{2K_{\mu}K_{\nu}-\eta_{\mu\nu}K^{2}-\eta_{\mu\nu}K\cdot P+(K_{\mu}P_{\nu}+P_{\mu}K_{\nu})}{K^{2}(P+K)^{2}}\right]\,.

Now in HTL approximation one can neglect external soft momentum
BP
;
Weldon:1982aq
;
Fradkin
;
KK
, i.e., one or higher power of
P
. So we get,

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle-8e^{2}\int\frac{d^{4}K}{(2\pi)^{4}}\left[\frac{K_{\mu}K_{\nu}}{K^{2}(P+K)^{2}}\right]+4e^{2}\eta_{\mu\nu}\int\frac{d^{4}K}{(2\pi)^{4}}\frac{1}{(P+K)^{2}}

(484)

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle-8e^{2}\int\frac{d^{4}K}{(2\pi)^{4}}\left[K_{\mu}K_{\nu}\Delta_{F}(K)\Delta_{F}(Q)\right]+4e^{2}\eta_{\mu\nu}\int\frac{d^{4}K}{(2\pi)^{4}}\Delta_{F}(Q)\,,

where
\Delta_{F}(K)=\frac{1}{K^{2}}
and
\Delta_{F}(Q)=\frac{1}{(P+K)^{2}}=\frac{1}{Q^{2}}
with
Q=P+K
. We also note that
F
stands for fermionic part of the propagator.

We take the time-time part of
\Pi_{\mu\nu}
as
Aritra
;
Bithika

\displaystyle\Pi_{00}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-8e^{2}T\sum\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\int\limits_{\{K\}}k_{0}^{2}\Delta_{F}(K)\Delta_{F}(Q)+4e^{2}T\sum\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\int\limits_{\{K\}}\Delta_{F}(Q)

(485)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-8e^{2}T\sum\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\int\limits_{\{K\}}(K^{2}+k^{2})\Delta_{F}(K)\Delta_{F}(Q)+4e^{2}T\sum\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\int\limits_{\{K\}}\Delta_{F}(Q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-4e^{2}T\sum\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\int\limits_{\{K\}}\Delta_{F}(K)-8e^{2}T\sum\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\int\limits_{\{K\}}k^{2}\Delta_{F}(K)\Delta_{F}(Q)\,,

where
\sum\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\int\limits_{\{K\}}
is a fermionic sum-integral and we have used
\Delta_{F}(Q)=1/(P+K)^{2}\approx 1/K^{2}=\Delta_{F}(K)
in the first term.

Also we obtain
Aritra
;
Bithika

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mu}^{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-8e^{2}T\sum\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\int\limits_{\{K\}}K^{2}\Delta_{F}(K)\Delta_{F}(Q)+4e^{2}\delta_{\mu}^{\mu}T\sum\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\int\limits_{\{K\}}\Delta_{F}(Q)

(486)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 8e^{2}T\sum\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\int\limits_{\{K\}}\Delta_{F}(Q)\approx 8e^{2}T\sum\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\int\limits_{\{K\}}\Delta_{F}(K).

We know the results of frequency sums:

\displaystyle T\sum_{k_{0}}\Delta_{F}(K)

\displaystyle=T\sum_{k_{0}}\frac{1}{K^{2}}=\frac{1}{2k}\left(1-2n_{F}(k)\right),

(487a)

\displaystyle T\sum_{k_{0}}\Delta_{F}(K)\Delta_{F}(Q)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4kq}\left[\left(1-n_{F}(k)-n_{F}(q)\right)\left(\frac{1}{\omega-k-q}-\frac{1}{\omega+k+q}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\left(n_{F}(k)-n_{F}(q)\right)\left(\frac{1}{\omega+k-q}-\frac{1}{\omega-k+q}\right)\right]\,,

(487b)

where
k=|\bm{\vec{k}}|
,
q=|\bm{\vec{k}+\vec{p}}|
and the Fermi distribution is given as
n_{F}(x)=1/(\exp(\beta x)+1)
.

Now using (
487a
) in (
486
), one gets

\Pi_{\mu}^{\mu}=8e^{2}\int\frac{d^{3}k}{(2\pi)^{3}}\frac{1}{2k}\left(1-2n_{F}(k)\right)\,.

(488)

Neglecting vacuum part, one can have

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mu}^{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{4e^{2}}{\pi^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}k\ n_{F}(k)\ dk

(489)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{4e^{2}}{\pi^{2}}\times\frac{\pi^{2}T^{2}}{12}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{e^{2}T^{2}}{3}=-m_{D}^{2}\,,

where the Debye mass in QED is given as
m_{D}^{2}=e^{2}T^{2}/3
.

Now using (
487a
) and (
487b
) in (
485
), one can write the time-time component as

\displaystyle\Pi_{00}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-4e^{2}\int\frac{d^{3}k}{(2\pi)^{3}}\frac{1}{2k}\left(1-2n_{F}(k)\right)-8e^{2}\int\frac{d^{3}k}{(2\pi)^{3}}\frac{k^{2}}{4kq}

(490)

\displaystyle\times\left[\left(1-n_{F}(k)-n_{F}(q)\right)\left(\frac{1}{\omega-k-q}-\frac{1}{\omega+k+q}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\left(n_{F}(k)-n_{F}(q)\right)\left(\frac{1}{\omega+k-q}-\frac{1}{\omega-k+q}\right)\right]\,.

Under the HTL approximation
BP
one can make following simplifications as

\displaystyle q\,=\,\left|\bm{\vec{p}}+\bm{\vec{k}}\right|

\displaystyle\,=\,\sqrt{p^{2}+k^{2}+2pk\cos\theta}=\sqrt{p^{2}+k^{2}+2pkc}\approx k+pc=k+{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}\,,

(491a)

\displaystyle n_{F}(q)

\displaystyle\,=n_{F}(k+{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}})\approx n_{F}(k)+{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}\frac{dn_{F}(k)}{dk}\,,

(491b)

\displaystyle\omega\pm k\pm q

\displaystyle\,=\omega\pm k\pm k\pm\,{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}\approx\pm 2k\,,

(491c)

\displaystyle\omega\pm k\mp q

\displaystyle\,=\omega\pm k\mp k\mp\,{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}\approx\omega\mp{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}\ .

(491d)

Now using (
491a
) to (
491d
) in (
487b
), one can write the time-time component as

\displaystyle\Pi_{00}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-4e^{2}\int\frac{d^{3}k}{(2\pi)^{3}}\frac{1}{2k}\left(1-2n_{F}(k)\right)-2e^{2}\int\frac{d^{3}k}{(2\pi)^{3}}\left[\left(1-2n_{F}(k)-{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}\frac{dn_{F}(k)}{dk}\right)\left(-\frac{1}{k}\right)\right.

(492)

\displaystyle\left.+{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}\frac{dn_{F}(k)}{dk}\left(\frac{1}{\omega-{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}}-\frac{1}{\omega+{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-4e^{2}\int\frac{d^{3}k}{(2\pi)^{3}}\frac{1}{2k}\left(1-2n_{F}(k)\right)+4e^{2}\int\frac{d^{3}k}{(2\pi)^{3}}\frac{1}{2k}\left(1-2n_{F}(k)\right)

\displaystyle-2e^{2}\int\frac{d^{3}k}{(2\pi)^{3}}\frac{{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}}{k}\frac{dn_{F}(k)}{dk}-2e^{2}\int\frac{d^{3}k}{(2\pi)^{3}}\frac{dn_{F}(k)}{dk}\left(\frac{{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}}{\omega-{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}}-\frac{{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}}{\omega+{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}}\right)

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle-2e^{2}\int\frac{d^{3}k}{(2\pi)^{3}}\frac{dn_{F}(k)}{dk}\left(\frac{{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}}{\omega-{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}}-\frac{{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}}{\omega+{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}}\right)\,,

where we have neglected the third term in second line for soft momentum. Now considering
\cos\theta=-\cos\theta
in the second term of the last line
we get

\displaystyle\Pi_{00}(\omega,p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2e^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{k^{2}\ dk}{2\pi^{2}}\frac{dn_{F}(k)}{dk}\int\frac{d\Omega}{4\pi}\frac{{2\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}}{\omega-{\bm{\vec{p}\cdot\hat{k}}}}

(493)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4e^{2}}{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{k^{2}\ n_{F}(k)(1-n_{F}(k))\ dk}{2\pi^{2}}\int\frac{d\Omega}{4\pi}\,\frac{\omega-P\cdot{\hat{K}}}{P\cdot{\hat{K}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4e^{2}}{2\pi^{2}T}\int_{0}^{\infty}k^{2}\ n_{F}(k)(1-n_{F}(k))\ dk\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{dc}{2}\,\left(\frac{\omega}{\omega-pc}-1\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4e^{2}}{2\pi^{2}T}\times\frac{\pi^{2}T^{3}}{6}\times\left[\frac{\omega}{2p}\ln\frac{\omega+p}{\omega-p}-1\right]=\frac{e^{2}T^{2}}{3}\left[\frac{\omega}{2p}\ln\frac{\omega+p}{\omega-p}-1\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m_{D}^{2}\left[\frac{\omega}{2p}\ln\frac{\omega+p}{\omega-p}-1\right]\,.

Using (
493
) in (
351
) one obtains the longitudinal component of the photon self-energy
Aritra
;
Bithika
as

\displaystyle\Pi_{L}(\omega,p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{P^{2}}{p^{2}}\Pi_{00}(\omega,p)

(494)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{m_{D}^{2}P^{2}}{p^{2}}\left[1-\frac{\omega}{2p}\ln\frac{\omega+p}{\omega-p}\right]\,.

Now using (
489
) and (
494
) in (
355
), one obtains the transverse component of the photon self-energy
Aritra
;
Bithika
as

\displaystyle\Pi_{T}(\omega,p)

\displaystyle=
